I guess… I just can’t make myself do anything anymore. I mostly sit in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I know that sounds bad, but it’s like everything takes too much. I used to knit sometimes, and journal, and I’d go walking—not far, just around, like past this house with a weird blue mailbox—but now I don’t even do that.

My mom keeps asking me what’s wrong, and I try to answer her, but it’s like I don’t have enough energy to get the words out. So I just kind of sit there.

Yeah… it’s changed. I don’t really get that feeling from them anymore. I don’t really… feel much pleasure from it now. Mostly I just sit in my room in the same clothes and I think about knitting or writing something down, but then I don’t do it. The scarf is still there, I think, or maybe it got moved—my mom moves bags when she cleans and then I get annoyed, but I don’t even really say anything.\n\nI used to walk too, just around the neighborhood, but now even that feels like too much. When my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer. It’s like I don’t have enough energy for words.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been… since I started that community-college program. So, like, a little over two years? Two years and maybe two months.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I thought it was just the classes being weird, or the bus schedule—there was this one stop where people would stand too close—but… yeah, since around then. That’s when everything started slipping.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I don’t think about it in a clear way like that, like making a plan or something—sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I just get really tired of everything being the same, and I’ll stare at the wall or the window and it’s like hours go by, but I don’t know if that counts as wishing I wasn’t alive. It’s more like… I don’t know what I’m supposed to be doing. My mom says I should open the curtains more because the room gets stale, and then I feel bad because she’s trying to help, but I just—yeah. I’m not sure how to answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Mostly it’s like I just can’t make myself do things anymore. I sit in my room in the same clothes, and I’ll think, okay, I should get up, I should maybe knit a little or write something in my journal, but then I just… don’t.\n\nAnd walking, too—I used to just go out, not even far, but now it feels like my body is heavy before I even start. My mom asks what’s wrong and I barely answer her, like even talking takes too much. I don’t know if that’s “moving slower,” exactly, but everything feels slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I don’t weigh myself much. My mom has a scale in the bathroom but it makes this clicking sound, or maybe that’s just the floor, and I usually avoid it.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I don’t know if that means weight or if I’m just wearing the same sweatpants too much. So… I haven’t noticed a big amount, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s more like… I don’t know what I’m supposed to be doing. My mom says I should open the curtains more because the room gets stale, and then I feel bad because she’s trying to help, but I just—yeah. I’m not sure how to answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Mostly it’s like I just can’t make myself do things anymore. I sit in my room in the same clothes, and I’ll think, okay, I should get up, I should maybe knit a little or write something in my journal, but then I just… don’t.\n\nAnd walking, too—I used to just go out, not even far, but now it feels like my body is heavy before I even start. My mom asks what’s wrong and I barely answer her, like even talking takes too much. I don’t know if that’s “moving slower,” exactly, but everything feels slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I don’t weigh myself much. My mom has a scale in the bathroom but it makes this clicking sound, or maybe that’s just the floor, and I usually avoid it.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I don’t know if that means weight or if I’m just wearing the same sweatpants too much. So… I haven’t noticed a big amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Over the past two years? I… I don’t really know. I wear a lot of the same loose things, like sweatpants and big shirts, so it’s hard to tell if they fit different or if they’re just stretched out.\n\nSometimes I think something feels tighter or looser, but then I get distracted because the laundry smells different depending on which detergent my mom buys, and then I don’t want to wear it anyway. I used to do more, I think… but now I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’m sleeping more exactly… I’m in my room a lot, so it probably looks like I’m resting. But sometimes I’m just sitting there in the same clothes, not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I’ll think I should knit or write in my journal, or maybe go for a walk because my mom says fresh air helps, and then I just… don’t. I’m not even always asleep. I just can’t make myself start. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, it feels like answering takes too much, so I barely say anything.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you still enjoy anything at all?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 19 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Negative_Symptoms\nmanifestation: \"Mostly I just can't make myself do anything anymore—I sit in my room with the same clothes on, don't knit or journal or go walking like I used to, and when my mom asks what's wrong I barely have the energy to answer.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know… not really like before. I’ll think maybe I could knit, because I used to do that, or write in my journal, but then the notebook just sits there. Sometimes the pen feels wrong in my hand anyway, like it’s too scratchy, and then I start thinking about where it came from.

Mostly I just stay in my room with the same clothes on and don’t do anything. My mom asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer, but it feels like even that is too much energy. So… I guess I don’t really enjoy much. I just kind of sit there.

Like with knitting, I used to like picking colors and making the rows even, and I had this one scarf I never finished—actually I think it’s still in a bag by my chair, unless my mom moved it when she was cleaning, she does that sometimes and then I can’t find anything.\n\nBut mostly I just don’t do it. Or journaling. I’ll think maybe I should write something, and then I just sit there. Same with walking. I don’t even really get dressed different most days. It all feels like too much, and when my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer because even talking feels… heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, or has that changed for you?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yeah. I don’t really… feel much pleasure from it now. Mostly I just sit in my room in the same clothes and I think about knitting or writing something down, but then I don’t do it. The scarf is still there, I think, or maybe it got moved—my mom moves bags when she cleans and then I get annoyed, but I don’t even really say anything.\n\nI used to walk too, just around the neighborhood, but now even that feels like too much. When my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer. It’s like I don’t have enough energy for words.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been… since I started that community-college program. So, like, a little over two years? Two years and maybe two months.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I thought it was just the classes being weird, or the bus schedule—there was this one stop where people would stand too close—but… yeah, since around then. That’s when everything started slipping.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I don’t think about it in a clear way like that, like making a plan or something—sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I just get really tired of everything being the same, and I’ll stare at the wall or the window and it’s like hours go by, but I don’t know if that counts as wishing I wasn’t alive. It’s more like… I don’t know what I’m supposed to be doing. My mom says I should open the curtains more because the room gets stale, and then I feel bad because she’s trying to help, but I just—yeah. I’m not sure how to answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Mostly it’s like I just can’t make myself do things anymore. I sit in my room in the same clothes, and I’ll think, okay, I should get up, I should maybe knit a little or write something in my journal, but then I just… don’t.\n\nAnd walking, too—I used to just go out, not even far, but now it feels like my body is heavy before I even start. My mom asks what’s wrong and I barely answer her, like even talking takes too much. I don’t know if that’s “moving slower,” exactly, but everything feels slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I don’t weigh myself much. My mom has a scale in the bathroom but it makes this clicking sound, or maybe that’s just the floor, and I usually avoid it.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I don’t know if that means weight or if I’m just wearing the same sweatpants too much. So… I haven’t noticed a big amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Over the past two years? I… I don’t really know. I wear a lot of the same loose things, like sweatpants and big shirts, so it’s hard to tell if they fit different or if they’re just stretched out.\n\nSometimes I think something feels tighter or looser, but then I get distracted because the laundry smells different depending on which detergent my mom buys, and then I don’t want to wear it anyway. So… I haven’t really noticed anything clear with my clothes, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or spending time with friends and family more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been. I don’t really go see people much now. Mostly I just stay in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I think about doing something—like knitting, or writing in my journal, or maybe going for a walk—but then I just don’t.\n\nMy mom comes in and asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer her properly, but it feels like even talking is too much. Sometimes I just say “nothing” or I don’t say much at all. I used to do more, I think… but now I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’m sleeping more exactly… I’m in my room a lot, so it probably looks like I’m resting. But sometimes I’m just sitting there in the same clothes, not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I’ll think I should knit or write in my journal, or maybe go for a walk because my mom says fresh air helps, and then I just… don’t. I’m not even always asleep. I just can’t make myself start. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, it feels like answering takes too much, so I barely say anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy anything at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like before. I’ll think maybe I could knit, because I used to do that, or write in my journal, but then the notebook just sits there. Sometimes the pen feels wrong in my hand anyway, like it’s too scratchy, and then I start thinking about where it came from.\n\nMostly I just stay in my room with the same clothes on and don’t do anything. My mom asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer, but it feels like even that is too much energy. So… I guess I don’t really enjoy much. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless or guilty about things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “worthless” is a big word. I don’t really sit there thinking that exact thing all day.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, like when my mom knocks and I don’t answer right, but then I start thinking about how the door sticks when it closes and how my dad was supposed to fix it, and it just goes somewhere else in my head. So I’m not sure. I don’t know if it’s most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I know everybody dies, right? Like my grandma used to say that, but she said it when she was watering the plants, which was weird because the plants were always dying anyway in those little brown pots.\n\nBut if you mean do I sit and plan something, I don’t think so. It’s more like the thought passes by sometimes, and then I’m thinking about the ceiling or the noise in the hallway. So… I’m not sure how often.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The scarf is still there, I think, or maybe it got moved—my mom moves bags when she cleans and then I get annoyed, but I don’t even really say anything.\n\nI used to walk too, just around the neighborhood, but now even that feels like too much. When my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer. It’s like I don’t have enough energy for words.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been… since I started that community-college program. So, like, a little over two years? Two years and maybe two months.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I thought it was just the classes being weird, or the bus schedule—there was this one stop where people would stand too close—but… yeah, since around then. That’s when everything started slipping.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I don’t think about it in a clear way like that, like making a plan or something—sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I just get really tired of everything being the same, and I’ll stare at the wall or the window and it’s like hours go by, but I don’t know if that counts as wishing I wasn’t alive. It’s more like… I don’t know what I’m supposed to be doing. My mom says I should open the curtains more because the room gets stale, and then I feel bad because she’s trying to help, but I just—yeah. I’m not sure how to answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Mostly it’s like I just can’t make myself do things anymore. I sit in my room in the same clothes, and I’ll think, okay, I should get up, I should maybe knit a little or write something in my journal, but then I just… don’t.\n\nAnd walking, too—I used to just go out, not even far, but now it feels like my body is heavy before I even start. My mom asks what’s wrong and I barely answer her, like even talking takes too much. I don’t know if that’s “moving slower,” exactly, but everything feels slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I don’t weigh myself much. My mom has a scale in the bathroom but it makes this clicking sound, or maybe that’s just the floor, and I usually avoid it.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I don’t know if that means weight or if I’m just wearing the same sweatpants too much. So… I haven’t noticed a big amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Over the past two years? I… I don’t really know. I wear a lot of the same loose things, like sweatpants and big shirts, so it’s hard to tell if they fit different or if they’re just stretched out.\n\nSometimes I think something feels tighter or looser, but then I get distracted because the laundry smells different depending on which detergent my mom buys, and then I don’t want to wear it anyway. So… I haven’t really noticed anything clear with my clothes, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or spending time with friends and family more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been. I don’t really go see people much now. Mostly I just stay in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I think about doing something—like knitting, or writing in my journal, or maybe going for a walk—but then I just don’t.\n\nMy mom comes in and asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer her properly, but it feels like even talking is too much. Sometimes I just say “nothing” or I don’t say much at all. I used to do more, I think… but now I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’m sleeping more exactly… I’m in my room a lot, so it probably looks like I’m resting. But sometimes I’m just sitting there in the same clothes, not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I’ll think I should knit or write in my journal, or maybe go for a walk because my mom says fresh air helps, and then I just… don’t. I’m not even always asleep. I just can’t make myself start. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, it feels like answering takes too much, so I barely say anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy anything at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like before. I’ll think maybe I could knit, because I used to do that, or write in my journal, but then the notebook just sits there. Sometimes the pen feels wrong in my hand anyway, like it’s too scratchy, and then I start thinking about where it came from.\n\nMostly I just stay in my room with the same clothes on and don’t do anything. My mom asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer, but it feels like even that is too much energy. So… I guess I don’t really enjoy much. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless or guilty about things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “worthless” is a big word. I don’t really sit there thinking that exact thing all day.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, like when my mom knocks and I don’t answer right, but then I start thinking about how the door sticks when it closes and how my dad was supposed to fix it, and it just goes somewhere else in my head. So I’m not sure. I don’t know if it’s most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I know everybody dies, right? So I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to, like knitting and journaling, or has that changed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed. I don’t really get that feeling from them anymore. Like with knitting, I used to like picking colors and making the rows even, and I had this one scarf I never finished—actually I think it’s still in a bag by my chair, unless my mom moved it when she was cleaning, she does that sometimes and then I can’t find anything.\n\nBut mostly I just don’t do it. Or journaling. I’ll think maybe I should write something, and then I just sit there. Same with walking. I don’t even really get dressed different most days. It all feels like too much, and when my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer because even talking feels… heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, or has that changed for you?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yeah. I don’t really… feel much pleasure from it now. Mostly I just sit in my room in the same clothes and I think about knitting or writing something down, but then I don’t do it. The scarf is still there, I think, or maybe it got moved—my mom moves bags when she cleans and then I get annoyed, but I don’t even really say anything.\n\nI used to walk too, just around the neighborhood, but now even that feels like too much. When my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer. It’s like I don’t have enough energy for words.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been… since I started that community-college program. So, like, a little over two years? Two years and maybe two months.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I thought it was just the classes being weird, or the bus schedule—there was this one stop where people would stand too close—but… yeah, since around then. That’s when everything started slipping.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I don’t think about it in a clear way like that, like making a plan or something—sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I just get really tired of everything being the same, and I’ll stare at the wall or the window and it’s like hours go by, but I don’t know if that counts as wishing I wasn’t alive. It’s more like… I don’t know what I’m supposed to be doing. My mom says I should open the curtains more because the room gets stale, and then I feel bad because she’s trying to help, but I just—yeah. I’m not sure how to answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Mostly it’s like I just can’t make myself do things anymore. I sit in my room in the same clothes, and I’ll think, okay, I should get up, I should maybe knit a little or write something in my journal, but then I just… don’t.\n\nAnd walking, too—I used to just go out, not even far, but now it feels like my body is heavy before I even start. My mom asks what’s wrong and I barely answer her, like even talking takes too much. I don’t know if that’s “moving slower,” exactly, but everything feels slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I don’t weigh myself much. My mom has a scale in the bathroom but it makes this clicking sound, or maybe that’s just the floor, and I usually avoid it.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I don’t know if that means weight or if I’m just wearing the same sweatpants too much. So… I haven’t noticed a big amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Over the past two years? I… I don’t really know. I wear a lot of the same loose things, like sweatpants and big shirts, so it’s hard to tell if they fit different or if they’re just stretched out.\n\nSometimes I think something feels tighter or looser, but then I get distracted because the laundry smells different depending on which detergent my mom buys, and then I don’t want to wear it anyway. So… I haven’t really noticed anything clear with my clothes, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or spending time with friends and family more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been. I don’t really go see people much now. Mostly I just stay in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I think about doing something—like knitting, or writing in my journal, or maybe going for a walk—but then I just don’t.\n\nMy mom comes in and asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer her properly, but it feels like even talking is too much. Sometimes I just say “nothing” or I don’t say much at all. I used to do more, I think… but now I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’m sleeping more exactly… I’m in my room a lot, so it probably looks like I’m resting. But sometimes I’m just sitting there in the same clothes, not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I’ll think I should knit or write in my journal, or maybe go for a walk because my mom says fresh air helps, and then I just… don’t. I’m not even always asleep. I just can’t make myself start. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, it feels like answering takes too much, so I barely say anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy anything at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like before. I’ll think maybe I could knit, because I used to do that, or write in my journal, but then the notebook just sits there. Sometimes the pen feels wrong in my hand anyway, like it’s too scratchy, and then I start thinking about where it came from.\n\nMostly I just stay in my room with the same clothes on and don’t do anything. My mom asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer, but it feels like even that is too much energy. So… I guess I don’t really enjoy much. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless or guilty about things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “worthless” is a big word. I don’t really sit there thinking that exact thing all day.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, like when my mom knocks and I don’t answer right, but then I start thinking about how the door sticks when it closes and how my dad was supposed to fix it, and it just goes somewhere else in my head. So I’m not sure. I don’t know if it’s most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I know everybody dies, right? Like my grandma used to say that, but she said it when she was watering the plants, which was weird because the plants were always dying anyway in those little brown pots.\n\nBut if you mean do I sit and plan something, I don’t think so. It’s more like the thought passes by sometimes, and then I’m thinking about the ceiling or the noise in the hallway. So… I’m not sure how often.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing things more slowly than you used to, or is it more that you just can't start doing them?\n\nPatient: It’s more… I can’t start, I think. Like I’m not exactly walking slow on purpose or anything. I just sit there, in my room, and the clothes are the same ones from before, and I know I should change them but then it feels like there are too many steps.\n\nI used to knit, and journal, and sometimes go walking, but now I just look at the stuff and don’t do it. The yarn gets all fuzzy if it’s been in the bag too long, and there was this blue one I liked but… yeah. I don’t know. I just can’t make myself begin. Even when my mom asks what’s wrong, answering feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body is moving more slowly than it used to, or is it just that you can't motivate yourself to start doing things?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the starting. My body isn’t like… dragging through mud exactly. I just sit in my room and nothing happens. Same clothes, same spot, and I know I should get up, change, do something.\n\nI used to knit and write in my journal, and I’d go walking sometimes, but now I just look at the yarn or the notebook and it feels too far away, even though it’s right there. My mom asks what’s wrong and I can barely answer her. Then I start thinking about how long the clothes have been on the chair, and the chair leg is uneven, and… yeah. I just can’t make myself do things.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if I would call it depressed exactly, but for more than two weeks I just haven’t really wanted to do anything. I mostly sit in my room with the same clothes on, and the laundry gets sort of piled there, and I keep thinking I should move it but then I don’t.

I used to knit, and write in my journal, and go for walks sometimes, but now I just… don’t. The notebook is still there, with this one page half-filled, and I can’t even make myself finish it. My mom asks me what’s wrong and I barely have the energy to answer her. So, yeah, I guess I lost interest in most things.

So… I haven’t really noticed anything clear with my clothes, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or spending time with friends and family more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I just can’t make myself do anything anymore. I mostly sit in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I know that sounds bad, but it’s like everything takes too much. I used to knit sometimes, and journal, and I’d go walking—not far, just around, like past this house with a weird blue mailbox—but now I don’t even do that.\n\nMy mom keeps asking me what’s wrong, and I try to answer her, but it’s like I don’t have enough energy to get the words out. So I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to, like knitting and journaling, or has that changed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed. I don’t really get that feeling from them anymore. Like with knitting, I used to like picking colors and making the rows even, and I had this one scarf I never finished—actually I think it’s still in a bag by my chair, unless my mom moved it when she was cleaning, she does that sometimes and then I can’t find anything.\n\nBut mostly I just don’t do it. Or journaling. I’ll think maybe I should write something, and then I just sit there. Same with walking. I don’t even really get dressed different most days. It all feels like too much, and when my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer because even talking feels… heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, or has that changed for you?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yeah. I don’t really… feel much pleasure from it now. Mostly I just sit in my room in the same clothes and I think about knitting or writing something down, but then I don’t do it. The scarf is still there, I think, or maybe it got moved—my mom moves bags when she cleans and then I get annoyed, but I don’t even really say anything.\n\nI used to walk too, just around the neighborhood, but now even that feels like too much. When my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer. It’s like I don’t have enough energy for words.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been… since I started that community-college program. So, like, a little over two years? Two years and maybe two months.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I thought it was just the classes being weird, or the bus schedule—there was this one stop where people would stand too close—but… yeah, since around then. That’s when everything started slipping.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I don’t think about it in a clear way like that, like making a plan or something—sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I just get really tired of everything being the same, and I’ll stare at the wall or the window and it’s like hours go by, but I don’t know if that counts as wishing I wasn’t alive. It’s more like… I don’t know what I’m supposed to be doing. My mom says I should open the curtains more because the room gets stale, and then I feel bad because she’s trying to help, but I just—yeah. I’m not sure how to answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Mostly it’s like I just can’t make myself do things anymore. I sit in my room in the same clothes, and I’ll think, okay, I should get up, I should maybe knit a little or write something in my journal, but then I just… don’t.\n\nAnd walking, too—I used to just go out, not even far, but now it feels like my body is heavy before I even start. My mom asks what’s wrong and I barely answer her, like even talking takes too much. I don’t know if that’s “moving slower,” exactly, but everything feels slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I don’t weigh myself much. My mom has a scale in the bathroom but it makes this clicking sound, or maybe that’s just the floor, and I usually avoid it.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I don’t know if that means weight or if I’m just wearing the same sweatpants too much. So… I haven’t noticed a big amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Over the past two years? I… I don’t really know. I wear a lot of the same loose things, like sweatpants and big shirts, so it’s hard to tell if they fit different or if they’re just stretched out.\n\nSometimes I think something feels tighter or looser, but then I get distracted because the laundry smells different depending on which detergent my mom buys, and then I don’t want to wear it anyway. So… I haven’t really noticed anything clear with my clothes, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or spending time with friends and family more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been. I don’t really go see people much now. Mostly I just stay in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I think about doing something—like knitting, or writing in my journal, or maybe going for a walk—but then I just don’t.\n\nMy mom comes in and asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer her properly, but it feels like even talking is too much. Sometimes I just say “nothing” or I don’t say much at all. I used to do more, I think… but now I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’m sleeping more exactly… I’m in my room a lot, so it probably looks like I’m resting. But sometimes I’m just sitting there in the same clothes, not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I’ll think I should knit or write in my journal, or maybe go for a walk because my mom says fresh air helps, and then I just… don’t. I’m not even always asleep. I just can’t make myself start. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, it feels like answering takes too much, so I barely say anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy anything at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like before. I’ll think maybe I could knit, because I used to do that, or write in my journal, but then the notebook just sits there. Sometimes the pen feels wrong in my hand anyway, like it’s too scratchy, and then I start thinking about where it came from.\n\nMostly I just stay in my room with the same clothes on and don’t do anything. My mom asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer, but it feels like even that is too much energy. So… I guess I don’t really enjoy much. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless or guilty about things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “worthless” is a big word. I don’t really sit there thinking that exact thing all day.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, like when my mom knocks and I don’t answer right, but then I start thinking about how the door sticks when it closes and how my dad was supposed to fix it, and it just goes somewhere else in my head. So I’m not sure. I don’t know if it’s most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I know everybody dies, right? What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I just can’t make myself do anything anymore. I mostly sit in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I know that sounds bad, but it’s like everything takes too much. I used to knit sometimes, and journal, and I’d go walking—not far, just around, like past this house with a weird blue mailbox—but now I don’t even do that.\n\nMy mom keeps asking me what’s wrong, and I try to answer her, but it’s like I don’t have enough energy to get the words out. So I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy the things you used to, like knitting and journaling, or has that changed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s changed. I don’t really get that feeling from them anymore. Like with knitting, I used to like picking colors and making the rows even, and I had this one scarf I never finished—actually I think it’s still in a bag by my chair, unless my mom moved it when she was cleaning, she does that sometimes and then I can’t find anything.\n\nBut mostly I just don’t do it. Or journaling. I’ll think maybe I should write something, and then I just sit there. Same with walking. I don’t even really get dressed different most days. It all feels like too much, and when my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer because even talking feels… heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, or has that changed for you?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yeah. I don’t really… feel much pleasure from it now. Mostly I just sit in my room in the same clothes and I think about knitting or writing something down, but then I don’t do it. The scarf is still there, I think, or maybe it got moved—my mom moves bags when she cleans and then I get annoyed, but I don’t even really say anything.\n\nI used to walk too, just around the neighborhood, but now even that feels like too much. When my mom asks what’s wrong, I barely answer. It’s like I don’t have enough energy for words.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been… since I started that community-college program. So, like, a little over two years? Two years and maybe two months.\n\nIt’s hard to pin down because at first I thought it was just the classes being weird, or the bus schedule—there was this one stop where people would stand too close—but… yeah, since around then. That’s when everything started slipping.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any thoughts about hurting yourself or wishing you weren't alive?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. I don’t think about it in a clear way like that, like making a plan or something—sorry, I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I just get really tired of everything being the same, and I’ll stare at the wall or the window and it’s like hours go by, but I don’t know if that counts as wishing I wasn’t alive. It’s more like… I don’t know what I’m supposed to be doing. My mom says I should open the curtains more because the room gets stale, and then I feel bad because she’s trying to help, but I just—yeah. I’m not sure how to answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Mostly it’s like I just can’t make myself do things anymore. I sit in my room in the same clothes, and I’ll think, okay, I should get up, I should maybe knit a little or write something in my journal, but then I just… don’t.\n\nAnd walking, too—I used to just go out, not even far, but now it feels like my body is heavy before I even start. My mom asks what’s wrong and I barely answer her, like even talking takes too much. I don’t know if that’s “moving slower,” exactly, but everything feels slower.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t really know. I don’t weigh myself much. My mom has a scale in the bathroom but it makes this clicking sound, or maybe that’s just the floor, and I usually avoid it.\n\nMy clothes maybe feel a little different sometimes, but I don’t know if that means weight or if I’m just wearing the same sweatpants too much. So… I haven’t noticed a big amount, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how your clothes fit over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Over the past two years? I… I don’t really know. I wear a lot of the same loose things, like sweatpants and big shirts, so it’s hard to tell if they fit different or if they’re just stretched out.\n\nSometimes I think something feels tighter or looser, but then I get distracted because the laundry smells different depending on which detergent my mom buys, and then I don’t want to wear it anyway. So… I haven’t really noticed anything clear with my clothes, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or spending time with friends and family more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been. I don’t really go see people much now. Mostly I just stay in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I think about doing something—like knitting, or writing in my journal, or maybe going for a walk—but then I just don’t.\n\nMy mom comes in and asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer her properly, but it feels like even talking is too much. Sometimes I just say “nothing” or I don’t say much at all. I used to do more, I think… but now I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’m sleeping more exactly… I’m in my room a lot, so it probably looks like I’m resting. But sometimes I’m just sitting there in the same clothes, not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I’ll think I should knit or write in my journal, or maybe go for a walk because my mom says fresh air helps, and then I just… don’t. I’m not even always asleep. I just can’t make myself start. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, it feels like answering takes too much, so I barely say anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy anything at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like before. I’ll think maybe I could knit, because I used to do that, or write in my journal, but then the notebook just sits there. Sometimes the pen feels wrong in my hand anyway, like it’s too scratchy, and then I start thinking about where it came from.\n\nMostly I just stay in my room with the same clothes on and don’t do anything. My mom asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer, but it feels like even that is too much energy. So… I guess I don’t really enjoy much. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless or guilty about things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “worthless” is a big word. I don’t really sit there thinking that exact thing all day.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, like when my mom knocks and I don’t answer right, but then I start thinking about how the door sticks when it closes and how my dad was supposed to fix it, and it just goes somewhere else in my head. So I’m not sure. I don’t know if it’s most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I know everybody dies, right? Like my grandma used to say that, but she said it when she was watering the plants, which was weird because the plants were always dying anyway in those little brown pots.\n\nBut if you mean do I sit and plan something, I don’t think so. It’s more like the thought passes by sometimes, and then I’m thinking about the ceiling or the noise in the hallway. So… I’m not sure how often.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing things more slowly than you used to, or is it more that you just can't start doing them?\n\nPatient: It’s more… I can’t start, I think. Like I’m not exactly walking slow on purpose or anything. I just sit there, in my room, and the clothes are the same ones from before, and I know I should change them but then it feels like there are too many steps.\n\nI used to knit, and journal, and sometimes go walking, but now I just look at the stuff and don’t do it. The yarn gets all fuzzy if it’s been in the bag too long, and there was this blue one I liked but… yeah. I don’t know. I just can’t make myself begin. Even when my mom asks what’s wrong, answering feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body is moving more slowly than it used to, or is it just that you can't motivate yourself to start doing things?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the starting. I wear a lot of the same loose things, like sweatpants and big shirts, so it’s hard to tell if they fit different or if they’re just stretched out.\n\nSometimes I think something feels tighter or looser, but then I get distracted because the laundry smells different depending on which detergent my mom buys, and then I don’t want to wear it anyway. So… I haven’t really noticed anything clear with my clothes, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or spending time with friends and family more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been. I don’t really go see people much now. Mostly I just stay in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I think about doing something—like knitting, or writing in my journal, or maybe going for a walk—but then I just don’t.\n\nMy mom comes in and asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer her properly, but it feels like even talking is too much. Sometimes I just say “nothing” or I don’t say much at all. I used to do more, I think… but now I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’m sleeping more exactly… I’m in my room a lot, so it probably looks like I’m resting. But sometimes I’m just sitting there in the same clothes, not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I’ll think I should knit or write in my journal, or maybe go for a walk because my mom says fresh air helps, and then I just… don’t. I’m not even always asleep. I just can’t make myself start. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, it feels like answering takes too much, so I barely say anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy anything at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like before. I’ll think maybe I could knit, because I used to do that, or write in my journal, but then the notebook just sits there. Sometimes the pen feels wrong in my hand anyway, like it’s too scratchy, and then I start thinking about where it came from.\n\nMostly I just stay in my room with the same clothes on and don’t do anything. My mom asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer, but it feels like even that is too much energy. So… I guess I don’t really enjoy much. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless or guilty about things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “worthless” is a big word. I don’t really sit there thinking that exact thing all day.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, like when my mom knocks and I don’t answer right, but then I start thinking about how the door sticks when it closes and how my dad was supposed to fix it, and it just goes somewhere else in my head. So I’m not sure. I don’t know if it’s most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I know everybody dies, right? Like my grandma used to say that, but she said it when she was watering the plants, which was weird because the plants were always dying anyway in those little brown pots.\n\nBut if you mean do I sit and plan something, I don’t think so. It’s more like the thought passes by sometimes, and then I’m thinking about the ceiling or the noise in the hallway. So… I’m not sure how often.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing things more slowly than you used to, or is it more that you just can't start doing them?\n\nPatient: It’s more… I can’t start, I think. Like I’m not exactly walking slow on purpose or anything. I just sit there, in my room, and the clothes are the same ones from before, and I know I should change them but then it feels like there are too many steps.\n\nI used to knit, and journal, and sometimes go walking, but now I just look at the stuff and don’t do it. The yarn gets all fuzzy if it’s been in the bag too long, and there was this blue one I liked but… yeah. I don’t know. I just can’t make myself begin. Even when my mom asks what’s wrong, answering feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body is moving more slowly than it used to, or is it just that you can't motivate yourself to start doing things?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the starting. My body isn’t like… dragging through mud exactly. I just sit in my room and nothing happens. Same clothes, same spot, and I know I should get up, change, do something.\n\nI used to knit and write in my journal, and I’d go walking sometimes, but now I just look at the yarn or the notebook and it feels too far away, even though it’s right there. My mom asks what’s wrong and I can barely answer her. Then I start thinking about how long the clothes have been on the chair, and the chair leg is uneven, and… yeah. I just can’t make myself do things.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if I would call it depressed exactly, but for more than two weeks I just haven’t really wanted to do anything. I mostly sit in my room with the same clothes on, and the laundry gets sort of piled there, and I keep thinking I should move it but then I don’t.\n\nI used to knit, and write in my journal, and go for walks sometimes, but now I just… don’t. The notebook is still there, with this one page half-filled, and I can’t even make myself finish it. My mom asks me what’s wrong and I barely have the energy to answer her. So, yeah, I guess I lost interest in most things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Um… weight? I don’t really know. I don’t check it much. We have a scale but it’s kind of shoved under the sink, and there’s all this cleaning stuff around it, so I don’t really take it out.\n\nI mean, my clothes feel… mostly the same, I think. Sometimes jeans feel weird but that might just be from washing them, or because I leave them on the chair too long and then they get that shape. So… I haven’t noticed a big change, not that I can say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think so? I haven’t weighed myself, so I can’t really say a number like that. Five percent sounds like math class, and I was never good at doing it in my head.\n\nMy clothes don’t seem suddenly loose or anything. The scale is still under the sink, I think, behind the bottles, and I don’t really pull it out. So… I haven’t noticed losing that much, but I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I wear a lot of the same loose things, like sweatpants and big shirts, so it’s hard to tell if they fit different or if they’re just stretched out.\n\nSometimes I think something feels tighter or looser, but then I get distracted because the laundry smells different depending on which detergent my mom buys, and then I don’t want to wear it anyway. So… I haven’t really noticed anything clear with my clothes, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or spending time with friends and family more than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been. I don’t really go see people much now. Mostly I just stay in my room, wearing the same clothes, and I think about doing something—like knitting, or writing in my journal, or maybe going for a walk—but then I just don’t.\n\nMy mom comes in and asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer her properly, but it feels like even talking is too much. Sometimes I just say “nothing” or I don’t say much at all. I used to do more, I think… but now I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're resting or sleeping more than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’m sleeping more exactly… I’m in my room a lot, so it probably looks like I’m resting. But sometimes I’m just sitting there in the same clothes, not really doing anything.\n\nLike, I’ll think I should knit or write in my journal, or maybe go for a walk because my mom says fresh air helps, and then I just… don’t. I’m not even always asleep. I just can’t make myself start. And when my mom asks what’s wrong, it feels like answering takes too much, so I barely say anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy anything at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really like before. I’ll think maybe I could knit, because I used to do that, or write in my journal, but then the notebook just sits there. Sometimes the pen feels wrong in my hand anyway, like it’s too scratchy, and then I start thinking about where it came from.\n\nMostly I just stay in my room with the same clothes on and don’t do anything. My mom asks what’s wrong, and I know I should answer, but it feels like even that is too much energy. So… I guess I don’t really enjoy much. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless or guilty about things most of the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “worthless” is a big word. I don’t really sit there thinking that exact thing all day.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, like when my mom knocks and I don’t answer right, but then I start thinking about how the door sticks when it closes and how my dad was supposed to fix it, and it just goes somewhere else in my head. So I’m not sure. I don’t know if it’s most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often think about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t know. I mean, I know everybody dies, right? Like my grandma used to say that, but she said it when she was watering the plants, which was weird because the plants were always dying anyway in those little brown pots.\n\nBut if you mean do I sit and plan something, I don’t think so. It’s more like the thought passes by sometimes, and then I’m thinking about the ceiling or the noise in the hallway. So… I’m not sure how often.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing things more slowly than you used to, or is it more that you just can't start doing them?\n\nPatient: It’s more… I can’t start, I think. Like I’m not exactly walking slow on purpose or anything. I just sit there, in my room, and the clothes are the same ones from before, and I know I should change them but then it feels like there are too many steps.\n\nI used to knit, and journal, and sometimes go walking, but now I just look at the stuff and don’t do it. The yarn gets all fuzzy if it’s been in the bag too long, and there was this blue one I liked but… yeah. I don’t know. I just can’t make myself begin. Even when my mom asks what’s wrong, answering feels like too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your body is moving more slowly than it used to, or is it just that you can't motivate yourself to start doing things?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the starting. My body isn’t like… dragging through mud exactly. I just sit in my room and nothing happens. Same clothes, same spot, and I know I should get up, change, do something.\n\nI used to knit and write in my journal, and I’d go walking sometimes, but now I just look at the yarn or the notebook and it feels too far away, even though it’s right there. My mom asks what’s wrong and I can barely answer her. Then I start thinking about how long the clothes have been on the chair, and the chair leg is uneven, and… yeah. I just can’t make myself do things.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed or lost interest in most things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t know if I would call it depressed exactly, but for more than two weeks I just haven’t really wanted to do anything. I mostly sit in my room with the same clothes on, and the laundry gets sort of piled there, and I keep thinking I should move it but then I don’t.\n\nI used to knit, and write in my journal, and go for walks sometimes, but now I just… don’t. The notebook is still there, with this one page half-filled, and I can’t even make myself finish it. My mom asks me what’s wrong and I barely have the energy to answer her. So, yeah, I guess I lost interest in most things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Um… weight? I don’t really know. I don’t check it much. We have a scale but it’s kind of shoved under the sink, and there’s all this cleaning stuff around it, so I don’t really take it out.\n\nI mean, my clothes feel… mostly the same, I think. Sometimes jeans feel weird but that might just be from washing them, or because I leave them on the chair too long and then they get that shape. So… I haven’t noticed a big change, not that I can say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think so? I haven’t weighed myself, so I can’t really say a number like that. Five percent sounds like math class, and I was never good at doing it in my head.\n\nMy clothes don’t seem suddenly loose or anything. The scale is still under the sink, I think, behind the bottles, and I don’t really pull it out. So… I haven’t noticed losing that much, but I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your movements have become slower than they used to be?\n\nPatient: Slower? I’m not sure… maybe a little, but I haven’t really been paying attention like that. Sometimes I feel like I take forever to get up or get started, but then I think maybe everyone feels like that when they’re tired.\n\nLike, this morning I was looking for one sock, and then I found this old receipt in the drawer from a store that closed, I think, and I started thinking about how weird it is when stores just disappear but the buildings stay there… sorry. Um. I don’t know if my movements are slower than before. I haven’t noticed someone saying that to me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes feeling looser on you over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Looser… um, I don’t think I’ve noticed that. Like my shirts and stuff still seem like mine, if that makes sense. Sometimes the waistband feels different, but then I think maybe it’s because I sit weird or because it got stretched out in the laundry.\n\nI haven’t really stood in the mirror checking. My closet’s kind of a mess anyway—there’s a hoodie in there I’ve had since school and one sleeve is all stretched because I used to pull it over my hand when it was cold. Sorry, that’s not really what you asked. No, I don’t think my clothes have been getting looser.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
